### Deploy record for S01 (PR #95, deploy run 88)

PR #95 ("step 1") was squash-merged to main as `e5d81ba`. Deploy run 88 succeeded and finished at 05:43:31 UTC on 24 October 2026. The pull request only added files and changed no existing one: documents, `src/masterpolicy.js` (imported by no live module at that time) and the static files under `public/brand/`, which no page links to. It has no switch.

### Deploy record for S02, S02A and S07 (PR #96, deploy run 89)

PR #96 ("step 2") was squash-merged to main as `3810c69`. Deploy run 89 succeeded and finished at 08:48:55 UTC. After the deploy, the six main pages and five APIs answered with headers identical to the snapshot taken before the deploy, the smoke test passed 14 of 14, and the phone and desktop check was OK. `wrangler.jsonc` was not changed, and every new switch (`BRAND`, `SITE_ORIGIN`, `SITE_ORIGINS`, `MAIN_TOKEN_V5` with its three values, `DEPLOYMENT_ENV`, `PUBLIC_V5`) is unset or off in production, so the live site behaves as `4aa408e` did. Nothing is activated.

The checks below were written before this merge. They stay the rule for every later deploy, because `wrangler.jsonc` keeps `"keep_vars": true`: a variable or secret set in the Cloudflare dashboard survives every deploy and the Worker reads it, while the page build reads only `wrangler.jsonc`. The switches-off parity above assumes none of the names these stages read exists in the dashboard. Before a deploy:

1. List the production Worker's secrets (`wrangler secret list`) and its dashboard variables (Workers & Pages, vicinity-map, Settings, Variables and Secrets). Read-only.
2. Confirm that none of these names is present, or that each is blank or `off`: `BRAND`, `SITE_ORIGIN`, `SITE_ORIGINS`, `MAIN_TOKEN_V5`, `PUBLIC_V5`, `MAIN_TOKEN_MINT`, `OFFICIAL_X_URL`, `OFFICIAL_TELEGRAM_URL`, `DEPLOYMENT_ENV`, `LEGACY_LINK_UNTIL`, `SELECTION_ARCHIVE_URL_A`, `SELECTION_ARCHIVE_URL_B`, and the switches of `src/masterpolicy.js` `FLAG_VALUES` (`WALLET_ONLY`, `LEGACY_LINK`, `LEGACY_SEATS`, `SELECTION_V5`, `FOUNDER_RECORD_CHAIN`, `ACTIVITY_POINTS`, `MANAGER_APPS`, `METADATA_V5`, `LAUNCH_V5`, `LEGACY_LAUNCH`, `CC_TRADING`, `CC_HOURLY`, `HOST_REDIRECT`). A switch name holding an off-like value outside its allowed set (for example `false`) changes no answer but logs one `policy config refused FLAG_VALUE_INVALID` line per isolate; a dashboard `MAIN_TOKEN_V5=on` or `BRAND` changes nothing (the switch needs the page build) and logs why; a dashboard `SITE_ORIGIN` no longer moves the old-host redirect; a dashboard `LEGACY_SEATS=frozen` (with `DEPLOYMENT_ENV`) freezes the seats but the pages keep today's copy until the switch is set in `wrangler.jsonc` and the pages are rebuilt (held, logged `LEGACY_SEATS_PAGES_NOT_BUILT`).
3. Record the result (names found, or "none") in the deploy pull request.

Keeping `LEGACY_LINK=on` with the passed date keeps the 410 answers instead; keeping only the date stops the build. Never remove `LEGACY_LINK_UNTIL` while `LEGACY_LINK=on`: the v5 check refuses `LEGACY_LINK` (`MISSING_LEGACY_LINK_UNTIL`) and the window is off; since the review this no longer switches `WALLET_ONLY` off. A later window (a new `LEGACY_LINK_UNTIL` ahead with `LEGACY_LINK=on`) lets a `closed_unresolved` account log in and link again.

A linked wallet stays linked, and the account signs in with it by today's signed login. A restricted session row (proof `legacy_link`) never counts as a session, whatever the switches say (review finding SEC-1): after a rollback the member signs in again by today's login. `users.legacy_state` and the `settings` row stay, unused; a `closed_unresolved` account cannot sign in by today's Google, e-mail or password login after a rollback either, until a later window links a wallet to it.

### Deploy checklist for S06 (before the merge goes live)

Items 1 and 2 are owner steps **before the merge** (S06 review, 25 Oct 2026). The owner's go of 25 Oct 2026 covers the merge; it does not
say whether these two were done, so the release step records the answer for each in the pull request.

1. **The deploy token's zone (E1), blocking.** `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` (GitHub, environment `production`, used by `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`) must be allowed to edit Workers routes on the citycoin.dev zone. As `docs/DEPLOY.md` step 1 set it up (template *Edit Cloudflare Workers*, zone resources `vicinity.city`) it is not, unless the owner widened it since (not readable from here). The owner edits the token (Cloudflare, My Profile, API Tokens, the deploy token, Edit): zone resources the citycoin.dev zone too (or all zones of the account), with the template's permissions, and checks that the citycoin.dev zone shows Active. Without it (wrangler 4.137, read in `node_modules/wrangler/wrangler-dist/cli.js`): the build and every test pass, the assets are uploaded and the new Worker version is deployed at 100%, so the settings and this pull request's code **are live** on vicinity.city; then the zone lookup for citycoin.dev finds nothing ("Could not find zone for `citycoin.dev`") or the custom-domain request is refused, wrangler exits 1, the run goes red and its live check does not run, and citycoin.dev still gets no DNS record. vicinity.city keeps serving (a failed run does not detach its custom domains; the new version is harmless there: `domain-cutover.md` section 2 item 2, status). Every later push to main then deploys and fails at the same step until the token gets the zone or the route is removed. After such a run: check `https://vicinity.city/` by hand, give the token the zone, re-run the workflow.
2. **The Google console (E12).** Add `https://citycoin.dev/api/auth/google/callback` to the Authorized redirect URIs of the OAuth client whose ID is `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` (Google Cloud Console, APIs & Services, Credentials). Adding it before the deploy is harmless. On citycoin.dev the Worker sends Google that return address (`test/s06-review.test.js`); with `SIGNUP_FLOW=v2` live and `WALLET_ONLY` off, Google is a main sign-up and sign-in path, so without the entry every Google tap on citycoin.dev (Continue with Google, Log in with Google, the Profile's Add) ends on Google's "Error 400: redirect_uri_mismatch" page. Nobody is signed in by mistake (a callback that reaches the other host answers `/connect?error=login_expired`), but Google sign-in does not work on citycoin.dev. **The owner's choice is not recorded yet.** If the owner declines, the S06 row records that Google sign-in on citycoin.dev is broken by design until S15 or S55; the alternative, hiding the Google button on hosts other than the canonical one, is a code change not made here.
3. **Dashboard variables (`keep_vars`).** List the production Worker's dashboard variables (Workers & Pages, vicinity-map, Settings, Variables and Secrets) and its secret names (`wrangler secret list`), read-only, and confirm that none of these is present (or each is blank or `off`): `SITE_ORIGIN`, `BRAND`, `MAIN_TOKEN_MINT`, `OFFICIAL_X_URL`, `OFFICIAL_TELEGRAM_URL`, `LEGACY_LINK_UNTIL`, `SELECTION_ARCHIVE_URL_A`, `SELECTION_ARCHIVE_URL_B`, and every switch of `src/masterpolicy.js` `FLAG_VALUES`: `WALLET_ONLY`, `LEGACY_LINK`, `LEGACY_SEATS`, `SELECTION_V5`, `FOUNDER_RECORD_CHAIN`, `ACTIVITY_POINTS`, `MANAGER_APPS`, `METADATA_V5`, `LAUNCH_V5`, `LEGACY_LAUNCH`, `CC_TRADING`, `CC_HOURLY`, `HOST_REDIRECT`, `PUBLIC_V5`, `MAIN_TOKEN_V5`. This matters more from S06 on: the committed `DEPLOYMENT_ENV=production` and `SITE_ORIGINS` supply the inputs those switches were refused for. Computed on the settings one switch at a time (the 5150529 settings against S06's): `WALLET_ONLY=on` alone goes from held (every sign-in 503) to fully live (Google, e-mail, password and tiny-transfer sign-in 404 for everyone; only the wallet challenge left); `PUBLIC_V5=on` counts; `LEGACY_LAUNCH=off` is accepted; `ACTIVITY_POINTS=shadow` opens the cc5-activity gate; `LEGACY_SEATS=frozen` is accepted by the check and stays held until the pages are built for it; `HOST_REDIRECT=on` stays refused. With none of them set every gate is the same on both settings. A dashboard `SITE_ORIGIN` of `https://citycoin.dev` would make it canonical before S55; any other value is refused and fails closed. The `wrangler.jsonc` values of `SITE_ORIGINS` and `DEPLOYMENT_ENV` replace any dashboard value of those two on deploy.
4. **What changes.** `wrangler.jsonc` (the route and the two settings); the Worker modules `src/official.js` (citycoin.dev on the official list), `src/mail.js`, `src/hostbind.js`, `src/auth.js`, `src/signup.js`, `src/legacy.js`, `src/pwlogin.js`, `src/walletlink.js` and `src/logins.js` (the e-mails name the host they were asked on); `public/_headers` (`noindex` on citycoin.dev). The build leaves `src/pages-built.js` and every built page as they are (section 11). On vicinity.city every answer is as after run 95 except `/api/official` (`websites` names citycoin.dev, `updated` 2026-10-25), the checker's verdict for citycoin.dev (official), and the host binding's exact Origin rule (`domain-cutover.md` section 1.2: a write whose Origin is spelled `http://vicinity.city`, `https://vicinity.city:443` or `https://VICINITY.CITY` now answers 403 `wrong_origin`; browsers never send those spellings, scripted clients might); its e-mails are byte for byte as before.
5. **After the deploy** (`domain-cutover.md` section 4, "After step 2"): `curl -sI https://citycoin.dev/` answers 200 over a certificate issued for citycoin.dev (issuance can take a few minutes after the attach) with `x-robots-tag: noindex`, and `https://vicinity.city/` without it; `node scripts/check-live.mjs https://citycoin.dev` passes (the workflow runs it for vicinity.city only; retry while the certificate is issued); `/api/health` answers 200 on citycoin.dev; `/api/check?q=citycoin.dev` answers official; a wallet sign-in works there; a `vs` cookie copied from vicinity.city reads signed out there; a POST with `Origin: https://vicinity.city` to citycoin.dev answers 403; a code e-mail asked on citycoin.dev names `https://citycoin.dev/connect`; Google sign-in on citycoin.dev comes back signed in (item 2 done) or shows `redirect_uri_mismatch` (not done); `www.citycoin.dev` still does not resolve; vicinity.city answers as in item 4 (`scripts/check-live.mjs` passes on it, run by the workflow when the deploy step succeeds).
6. **Rollback** (`domain-cutover.md` section 5): one pull request that sets `SITE_ORIGINS` to `https://vicinity.city` (change the value, never delete the line: `keep_vars`) and removes the citycoin.dev route; after its deploy, confirm in the dashboard (Workers & Pages, the Worker, Settings, Domains & Routes) that citycoin.dev is detached, and detach it by hand if not. Nothing in D1 needs undoing: the `host` columns stay, nullable and unread. The review fixes need no rollback: unbound, the e-mails name vicinity.city again, the `noindex` rule applies only on citycoin.dev, and citycoin.dev stays on the official list (the owner's zone).

### Gate for the S15 switch-on (from the S09-S14 review)

1. Copy (review finding F3). With the switch on, `/cities` still lists the founder requirement as "Wallet + Google or e-mail", `/rules` still says "One account per wallet and per Google login or verified e-mail" and refers to check-in coordinates, and `/terms` still says a member may link a Google account or a verified e-mail address. Before `WALLET_ONLY=on`: the cities requirement copy is switched (read through `/api/me`, or by S16), and the Rules and Terms texts are revised and approved by the owner (E12). The fixer did not write these texts: they are claims only the owner may make.
2. Log lines (PAR-02). With `WALLET_ONLY` or `LEGACY_LINK` set but refused, the Worker logs once per isolate at `console.error`: `wallet-only switch refused WALLET_ONLY_SITE_REFUSED`, `wallet-only switch refused WALLET_ONLY_POLICY_REFUSED`, `legacy link switch refused LEGACY_LINK_WALLET_ONLY_OFF`, `legacy link switch refused LEGACY_LINK_POLICY_REFUSED`, `legacy link switch refused LEGACY_LINK_UNTIL_PASSED`. Never with the switches unset. Since the review (SEC-1) a `wallet-only switch refused` line means the Worker is HELD: sign-in, registration and pairing answer 503 until the configuration is fixed. Alerts should page on it during and after the switch-on, and not before it.
3. Wallet universal links (SEC-8). The "Open app" button encodes the whole hand-off address, `#register=<code>` included, into the wallet vendor's https link (`https://phantom.com/ul/browse/...`, Solflare, Backpack). When the phone does not hand the link to the app, the vendor's web server receives the live code. Phantom, Solflare and Backpack are therefore trusted parties for the five minutes a code lives; what protects the code is its single use, its five-minute life and the opener and network binding. The code comments and `schema-and-api.md` no longer claim that codes never reach a server log. Keeping the code out of the vendor link (open the app at a wait page, fetch the code after the check number) is an optional later change.
4. Cookie names (SEC-9, a recorded deviation, not applied). The session cookie `vs` and the sign-up cookie `vsu` keep their names under `WALLET_ONLY` (host-only, no `Domain`, but not `__Host-` prefixed; only the new login-pairing nonce is `__Host-vpr`). Until they are renamed (issue `__Host-vs` and `__Host-vsu` under the switch and read the old names only for sessions made before it, as R1-01 planned), no host under citycoin.dev or vicinity.city other than the allowlisted ones may be served by a third party or used for a preview: a sibling subdomain could set a parent-domain cookie of the same name.
5. Moderator wording (PAR-03, optional). `nearWords` now says "registered in X, not location-checked" for a `town_requests` row with `inside = 0`, a `near_name` and no `near_km`, which today's code cannot write. A read-only count on production D1 confirms it is unobservable: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM town_requests WHERE near_name IS NOT NULL AND near_km IS NULL AND inside = 0` (expected 0).
6. Not part of S09 to S15 (PAR-05): the full `GET /api/me` answers 500 for a wallet member when every Solana RPC fails (src/chain.js, identical before this work). A separate change may mark the balance unavailable instead.
7. Contact e-mail (F1). Under the switch the dashboard no longer offers Add or Change for the contact e-mail (only Remove of an existing one), because `/api/auth/email/start` is gone. The R1-30 contact-only sender (`/api/me/contact/email/start`: signed in, per-user limit, never a login) is not built; until it is, members cannot add a contact e-mail.

**Status (go-live pull request, not merged; owner's go of 25 Oct 2026):** item 1 is built, and items 3 and 4 are accepted deviations. Item 2 is in the go-live deploy checklist. Items 5 to 7 are unchanged. Section 11, "Go-live switch-on", says where each item stands.

### Rebrand stage: the S48 labels behind `BRAND=citycoin` (branch `cc/rebrand` on `46c5825`; this pull request, not merged, switched off)

**Owner directive (24 October 2026), quoted as written:** "there should be no existience of vicinty. only citycoin." The same message asked: "also when should i expect to see thing live on citycoin.dev?" The answer is the switch-on order at the end of this subsection. This report gives no dates.

| Stage | Pull request | Switch | Live site | Checks | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **S48** (labels) Citycoin.dev rebrand | **Built in this pull request, switched off.** Branch `cc/rebrand`: five area commits (pages, client scripts, Worker texts, and two for the legacy token and the official accounts), rebased onto `edabab5`, then onto `5150529` (with the review fixes and the final check's follow-ups), then, as one commit, onto `4a50794` (S23), followed by this report and the second check's follow-ups, and then, all seven commits as they were, onto `46c5825` (S06, PR #104) (section 13 lists the commits). Not merged. | `BRAND=citycoin`, unset in production. It can only be set in one configuration pull request together with `LEGACY_SEATS=frozen`, `DEPLOYMENT_ENV` and an `EMAIL_FROM` without the old name; otherwise the page build refuses it (`scripts/pages/build.mjs` `pageBrandPrerequisites`). | **No change at merge.** With `BRAND` unset, the 16 built pages and `src/pages-built.js` are byte-identical to `46c5825` (main, S06 included), and every answer, D1 row, log line and e-mail is main's on vicinity.city and on citycoin.dev (S06's e-mails link to the host they were asked on). Two things do ship. First, 23 client scripts change bytes (P1): 21 carry BRAND-only branches that today's visitors download but never run, and `public/theme.js` and `public/official.js` carry the review fixes F8 and F9, which act only on a page built for another label set. Second, two unlinked static files appear: `/brand/citycoin.css` and `/brand/citycoin.webmanifest` (P2). When switched on (pages built with it): no "vicinity" in any case form anywhere a person can see. The one exception is the serving host, while vicinity.city still serves the site (below). | Advances D01, V05, V06 | S02, S02A, S16 |

**What is built.**
1. Pages. Every page source carries brand blocks or `{{productName}}`. A build with `BRAND=citycoin` stops on any page that still says "vicinity" in any case form. The pages get the Citycoin.dev lockup, favicon set and `data-brand` attributes on `<html>`.
2. Client scripts. One shared `brand` helper in the 21 scripts reads those attributes. On today's pages it returns each text exactly as written.
3. Worker texts (`src/brandwords.js`). E-mails, wallet statements, API messages, admin texts and the metadata of new coins name Citycoin.dev, but only while the deployed pages were built with that label set.
4. The legacy token. Under `BRAND`, with no verified main token, no token is live. No address, price, Buy, holder list, ranks or balance is shown, and `/token` is a plain "not live yet" page. The official list, link checker, coin page, portfolio, swap, Send and `/api/policy` leave out `$VICINITY`, its team wallets, its lock and the former X account. Nobody's holdings are swapped or touched.
5. Official accounts. Under `BRAND`, only what `OFFICIAL_X_URL` and `OFFICIAL_TELEGRAM_URL` hold counts. No footer and no list on `/official` shows an account until `MAIN_TOKEN_V5` counts, because the X address `@CityCoinDev` has an incomplete verification record (`amendment-01-new-main-token.md` section 4.4). Asked about such a configured account, the link checker says the team named it and its verification is not finished (a warning: "Final check follow-ups" below).

**Review findings and what was done.** Each finding was reproduced first. The regression tests are in `test/rebrand-review-fixes.test.js` (11 new tests), with changed cases in `test/brand-adapters.test.js`, `test/build-parity.test.js` and five other test files.

| Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|

Under `BRAND`, while the serving host is vicinity.city (`SITE_ORIGIN` unset), `/coin-meta/<mint>.json` leaves out `external_url`, `website`, `createdOn` and `launchpadLogo`. Only the `image` prefix compiled into the deployed program remains, and that is out of scope. The JSON is cached for 5 minutes, and it is rebuilt once from the coin's row when `SITE_ORIGIN` names a host other than the old ones (`src/coinmeta.js`; `src/brandwords.js` `onLegacyHost`). With `BRAND` unset the JSON is today's, stored for good. |

The dashboard, map and admin scripts treat a page built for another label set as frozen, so they show no holding bar, founder amount or grace top-up, whatever `/api/me` says. While no token is live, `/api/me` also leaves out the token badges (holder, founder-ready, big holder, top 100, top 10) (`src/me.js`). |

Both gave the same words for every label set ("No Vicinity fee." byte for byte with `BRAND` unset or with pages not built for the set; "No Citycoin.dev fee." under the Citycoin.dev set), so no answer changes. S39's test (`test/free-transfer.test.js`) keeps its intent and checks the same wordings through `bw`, and its source check of the route now reads `bw: brandWords(env)` (as S39's own merge note foresaw). |

The rebrand's coin art for the Citycoin.dev label set (t1 with "CITYCOIN.DEV" on the arc; same numbers, colours, fonts and renderer as t1) is now `t1c`, never `t2`. Why that id: a template id is stored with every picture (`coin_art.template_version`) and in every coin version's content (`image.template`), and is immutable, so one id may never name two pictures; S22 (merged) gave `t2` to a different picture, the new program's coin art (`src/coinart-t2.js`, Civic Atlas colours and emblem), and keeps `t3` for its next version. `t1c` follows the id format ("t", the version's number) with a letter for a variant of t1, which keeps it out of S22's numbered sequence for good. The picture's bytes are unchanged (the same golden SVG and PNG hashes as before, now under `t1c`). S22's pins are relaxed from "`src/coinart.js` holds t1 only" to "t1 is byte for byte `edabab5`'s and the default, no id there is S22's, and S22's t2 is untouched" (`test/coinart-t2.test.js`, `test/coinmeta-v2.test.js`); `test/coinart.test.js` adds a check of every id against S22's and shows that S22's t2 and `t1c` are different pictures. `src/coinart-t2.js` is not wired and not edited. Nothing stored changes: no coin was drawn with either id under `BRAND=citycoin`, which is off. |
| 2 (medium): the CI trial turns red once the switch is committed | Fixed. `scripts/brand/switch-trial.mjs` now reads the committed `wrangler.jsonc` first. When it already carries `BRAND=citycoin` with its prerequisites (the page build's own checks: `LEGACY_SEATS=frozen` with an accepted `DEPLOYMENT_ENV`, an `EMAIL_FROM` without the old name, no v5 setting refused), it writes nothing, prints one line ("switch-trial: wrangler.jsonc already sets the switch with its prerequisites (…): nothing to try and nothing written; the build that follows builds and tests these committed settings") and exits 0, so the configuration pull request and every later one stay green. It still exits 1, with the build's reason, on a half-set switch (`BRAND=citycoin` without the freeze, without `DEPLOYMENT_ENV`, or with today's sender), a `BRAND` naming another label set, a `LEGACY_SEATS` the switch cannot carry, any setting the page build refuses, and the trial's own placeholder sender in the committed file (its result applied twice or committed). Prerequisites committed before `BRAND` (the freeze alone, a `DEPLOYMENT_ENV` set for another switch, the owner's new sender, a blank `BRAND` line) are kept, and the switch is tried on top of them. Tests: three in `test/rebrand-review-fixes.test.js`, each also running the script on a scratch file (`--wrangler`, a test-only option) for its exit code and output. |
| 3 (low): the checker calls the configured X account "not official" | Fixed, as a choice the owner can revisit. Under `BRAND=citycoin` without the main-token switch, `/official#check` (GET `/api/check`) answered the configured but not yet verified https://x.com/CityCoinDev as "Not official … not on the official list". An account the configuration names (`OFFICIAL_X_URL`, `OFFICIAL_TELEGRAM_URL`) that is not on the official list yet now gets a neutral warning (the page shows "Be careful"), never "official" and never "not official": "The Citycoin.dev team named this X account (@CityCoinDev), but its verification is not finished yet, so this checker cannot confirm it yet. Never share your recovery phrase or private key with any account." (`src/official.js` `unverifiedAnswer`, with `unverified: true` in the answer). It covers the account's own links (x.com, twitter.com, www and mobile forms, any letter case) and its @handle; any other account or link answers as before. The account is still shown nowhere (no footer, no list) until `MAIN_TOKEN_V5` counts, the day its verification is finished; then the answer is "official", as S02A has it. With `BRAND` unset the checker never reads these settings and every answer is today's, word for word. Tests: one new and one changed in `test/brand-adapters.test.js` (both failed on the code before the change). |
| Found by the parity sweep after the rebase (not a finding of the final check) | Fixed. With `BRAND` unset, a stored coin JSON (`/coin-meta/<mint>.json`) without the legacy brand's `launchpad` and `createdOn` fields read as a CRAWL-1 provisional JSON and was served with `max-age=300` instead of today's `max-age=3600` (same body). Every JSON the Worker stores today has both fields, so no live coin was affected, but the answer was not today's for every possible row. `src/coinmeta.js` now looks for a provisional JSON only under another label set; with today's label set every stored JSON is served exactly as at `5150529`. Regression test in `test/rebrand-review-fixes.test.js` (it failed before the change). |

The suite's stand-in for today's site (`test/helpers/today-build.mjs` `todayWrangler`) now first takes back exactly what the CI trial wrote, read from the trial's own comment line in the runner's copy (`scripts/brand/wrangler-vars.mjs` `undoTrial`, which gives back the committed bytes), and then only the switch's own settings: `BRAND` when set, `LEGACY_SEATS` when frozen, `MAIN_TOKEN_V5` when on, and today's sender when a label set was set. It never strips a committed `DEPLOYMENT_ENV`, `SITE_ORIGIN` or `SITE_ORIGINS`. The trial-test fixtures write their settings by place over whatever the committed file holds, and each case runs on today's file and on S06's (`SITE_ORIGINS` with citycoin.dev, `DEPLOYMENT_ENV` production). Run on the merge of this branch with `cc/s06` as it was then (`11a6e4b`), both CI jobs passed. The switch trial of the rebased branch then caught the four new stand-in tests reading the trial's copy as the committed file (4 failures in that job only); they now read it through the stand-in, so they hold in the trial job and once the switch is committed. S06 has since moved to `530774b` (on `4a50794`, with its review fixes). A trial merge with it (`git merge-tree` in a temporary object store; no branch moved) has five textual conflicts: S06 gives the three e-mails of `src/mail.js` an `origin` parameter (the host the request came in on, its finding R3) where this branch gives them `env` (the label set), the three call sites in `src/auth.js`, `src/logins.js` and `src/walletlink.js` follow, and this report. None is a stand-in failure: S06's `DEPLOYMENT_ENV` and `SITE_ORIGINS` stay as committed in both jobs. One consequence is recorded in the switch-on order: a configuration pull request that sets `DEPLOYMENT_ENV`, `SITE_ORIGIN` or `SITE_ORIGINS` for the first time updates the suite's pins of those committed values itself, as S06 does. With S06 merged first the `BRAND` pull request needs no test change, unless it also sets `SITE_ORIGIN`. Whichever of S06 and this branch merges second runs both jobs again. S06 merged first (PR #104, `46c5825`): the rebase onto it met the five conflicts of the trial and updated the three pins (the note after the S23 note), and both jobs pass on the rebased head (section 11 tests table). |

The rebase onto S23 (`4a50794`, PR #103; it deletes `src/lpbatch.js` and `COIN_V2_BATCH` and adds `LEGACY_LAUNCH`). The 13 commits on `5150529` were first squashed into two (the code with its tests, then the report), kept as they were on local branch `cc/rebrand-pre-rebase-fe368bc`, and the four second-check commits followed. Every conflict keeps S23's behaviour and words and adds this branch's label wrapping. `src/lpbatch.js` stays deleted, with this branch's edits to it. In `src/coinready.js`, `coinGate` takes S23's `legacy_launch_closed` gate in the label set's words. In `src/coinreview.js` (8 hunks), S23's city-list words (`registryWords`, the approved text, the notification, the closed review) go through `bw`, and S23's private `selectionProblem` stays private. `src/lpadmin.js` `candidateList`, `src/coins.js` and `src/lplaunch.js` take both import lists. `public/coin-ready.js`, `public/coin-review.js`, `public/dashboard.js` (6 hunks), `public/dashboard-roles.js` (4) and `public/dashboard-v2.js` keep S23's closed-launch branches (`launchClosed`, `LAUNCH_CLOSED`, `launchShut`) wrapped in the client's `brand` tag, with the label set's unit. The RB-1 commit met the same files again (the coin studio and the founder steps hidden for both reasons). S23 changed today's Worker words on purpose (the deleted round's gates, the coin review's city-list words), so the fingerprint in `test/build-parity.test.js` was computed again from the `4a50794` tree with the same calls: `f850adc9…`. The `c5df86a` and `5150529` trees both give the old `4dd8a201…`. The separate pin of `src/lpbatch.js`'s checks went with that file. Every S23 line that says "Vicinity" or `$VICINITY` is wrapped (`git diff 5150529 4a50794` read line by line), and the trial build has no page that says "vicinity".

The rebase onto S06 (`46c5825`, PR #104: citycoin.dev in `wrangler.jsonc` beside vicinity.city, `SITE_ORIGINS` and `DEPLOYMENT_ENV` committed, and three Worker fixes for two hosts). The seven commits were rebased as they were; the head before is kept on local branch `cc/rebrand-pre-rebase-e52fe0d`. The conflicts were the five the S06-CI-RED trial found, and both behaviours are kept. The three e-mails of `src/mail.js` take S06's origin (the host the request was served as, `src/hostbind.js` `mailOrigin`) as their third argument and this branch's `env` (the label set) as their fourth: `verificationEmail(code, kind, origin, env)`, `walletLinkedEmail(masked, app, origin, env)` and `googleAddedEmail(origin, env)`. With today's label set they are S06's e-mails byte for byte: links to vicinity.city when no origin is given or the request came in on vicinity.city, and to citycoin.dev when it came in on citycoin.dev. Under `BRAND=citycoin` the words are the label set's and never write a host out, and the one button opens the served origin, or, with no usable origin, the configured canonical one. The three call sites (`src/auth.js` `sendCodeEmail`, `src/logins.js` `googleNotice`, `src/walletlink.js` `linkNotice`) pass both. S06's citycoin.dev on `OFFICIAL.websites` stays: under `BRAND=citycoin` the checker calls citycoin.dev and its www. form the official website, as today's label set does since S06, and the planned-address answer (F9) is reached only for a list without it. `src/official.js`, `public/_headers`, `wrangler.jsonc` and S06's tests merged without a conflict. Three pins that S06 moves on purpose were updated, as the S06-CI-RED row foresaw: the count of "vicinity" in `src/mail.js` (`test/brand-adapters.test.js`, 21 to 18), the checker's answer for citycoin.dev under `BRAND` (same file: now "official", with the planned answer checked on a list without citycoin.dev), and the legacy-answers fingerprint (`test/build-parity.test.js`), computed again with the same probe from a `git archive` of `46c5825` (`aa2d68a2…`; the same probe on `4a50794` gives the old `a58d2745…`). The Worker-words fingerprint stays `f850adc9…` (the same on `46c5825`). The rebrand tests call the e-mails with the new argument order, and one new test checks the label set's e-mails with S06's origins. With `BRAND` unset the built pages and `src/pages-built.js` are `46c5825`'s byte for byte, and the parity sweep on both served hosts found every answer, e-mail, job result and D1 row equal to `46c5825`'s apart from the 23 scripts' bytes (section 11 tests table).


**Go-live review follow-ups on the label set (25 October 2026, on `d7e62ca`).** The review of the go-live branch (`cc/golive`, which sits on this one) found four defects whose cause is in this branch's `BRAND=citycoin` behaviour. They are fixed here, behind the label set, so `BRAND` unset stays today's site. Each was reproduced first on `d7e62ca`. The regression tests are in `test/rebrand-golive-review.test.js` (8 new), with one changed case in `test/brand-adapters.test.js`. The go-live findings that belong to the switch-on itself are handled on `cc/golive` (its subsection in the go-live report). Parity with `BRAND` unset was proved again after these fixes. The page build gives the same `public/` and `src/pages-built.js` as before, so the built pages still equal `46c5825`'s. A real-Worker sweep of `46c5825` against this tree compared 1,821 answers per run on vicinity.city: every public file by GET and HEAD, all 186 `/api` route literals of `46c5825` signed out under five methods, signed-in reads and writes for four members, the coin metadata paths, the swap quote and build, and three job runs, with every D1 row, the schema, every log line and every Jupiter request. It ran in four scenarios: committed, bare, the go-live settings without `BRAND`, and `OFFICIAL_X_URL` with the freeze. Each run differed only in the bytes of the 23 client scripts (P1 above), and in nothing else. A second run of `46c5825` alone was identical to the first.

| Finding | Outcome |
|---|---|
| GL-1 (high): under `BRAND=citycoin` anyone could take the name "CityCoinDev" (the configured X handle) or "Citycoin" | Fixed. Reproduced: `reservedUsername("CityCoinDev")`, `("citycoin")`, `("Citycoin_dev")`, `("CitycoinTeam")` and `("C1tyc0in")` were all false. Under a label set other than today's, `src/me.js` `brandReserve` adds two rules to the reserved names: anything that looks like the label set's own words (`src/brandwords.js` `ownWords`: "citycoin" and "citycoindev", with look-alike letters, the same rule as "vicinity"), and exactly the handle of each official account the configuration names (`OFFICIAL_X_URL`, `OFFICIAL_TELEGRAM_URL`). Both name checks use it: the display name at wallet-only registration (`src/register.js`) and `/api/me/username`. The label set is the Worker's reading (`brandOf`), so pages built with the Citycoin.dev labels keep these names reserved even if `BRAND` goes missing. The coin screener's staff rule ("Vicinity" next to a staff word, a flag for a person) also counts the label set's words when the context carries them (`src/coinreview.js` passes `brandNames`; `src/screen.js`). The lists' rules version is unchanged, because the rule only flags and a person reads every founder text anyway. With `BRAND` unset nothing more is reserved and the screener's context is unchanged. Not done: reserving these names on today's site as well, as the finding suggested ("whatever BRAND says"). That would change today's answers and break this branch's parity rule. Instead, the go-live deploy checklist has a read-only check that no existing username matches the new rules before the merge. |
| GL-8 (low): the checker called unattached or soon-removed hosts "the official Citycoin.dev website" | Fixed. Under a label set other than today's, the link checker (`src/official.js` `checkOfficialV5`) says "official" only for a host this Worker serves in place (`SITE_ORIGINS`), compared exactly as written, so www. counts as a host of its own. Any other website name of ours, with or without www. (the published list, the legacy hosts and the cutover's own: www.citycoin.dev, www.vicinity.city, vicinitycity.net, vicinitycity.com), gets the warning "This is an address of ours, but it does not serve the Citycoin.dev site, so nothing there is official." citycoin.dev, while `SITE_ORIGINS` does not list it, keeps the "planned address" answer. vicinity.city stays official while `SITE_ORIGINS` lists it, which S55 changes. Look-alikes stay "not official". With `BRAND` unset every website answer is today's. |
| GL-HON-7 (low): the `$VICINITY` balance history would be pruned within 45 days of the switch | Fixed. Under a label set other than today's without `MAIN_TOKEN_V5`, nothing is sampled (`maybeSample`: `not_launched`), so `src/ledger.js` `pruneLedger` now forgets nothing either. The `day:` blobs and the `balance_samples` rows stay whole, as history, until a main token counts. With `BRAND` unset, and under `MAIN_TOKEN_V5`, the prune is exactly as before. |
| GL-DEP-8 (low): the fee words and the official-coins list were kept at the edge under keys that ignore the label set | Fixed. `/api/fees` and `/api/official-coins` (and the CSV) now append `mainCacheTag(env)` to their edge keys, as the coins, members, seats and swap-token answers already did. It is "" with today's label set, so today's keys stay the same byte for byte. Under the Citycoin.dev labels it is `-nolegacy`, so an answer kept before a label-set change, or before a rollback, is never served after it. |
| RB-P1 (low): `public/brand/citycoin.css` and `citycoin.webmanifest` answer 200 with `BRAND` unset | Accepted, as P2 above already records. No page built with `BRAND` unset links either file. Every CSS rule is scoped to `:root[data-brand="citycoin"]`, and other Citycoin.dev brand files were already public on main. |
| RB-P2, RB-P3 (nits): the client scripts' bytes and the Worker with `BRAND` unset | No change needed. These are the review's parity record: the built pages and `src/pages-built.js` are byte-identical to `46c5825`, and the real-Worker sweep found no difference on either host. |
| RB-ENV (nit): the reviewer's scratch output briefly filled the shared disk | Not a defect of this branch. Nothing here depends on it, and this round wrote only small files: the base tree and compact sweep outputs in the scratchpad's `gl-fix-tmp`, which were deleted afterwards. |

**Out of scope, and why.** No Citycoin.dev page shows anything on this list. Each item is either never visible or lives outside the website's code.
- Internal names nobody sees: storage keys, cookie names (`vs`, `vsu`), CSS class names, function and variable names, comments, D1 table and column names, JSON keys the pages do not display (`/api/launchpad`, `/api/coins`, `/api/fees`), hash domains, the mail MIME boundary, and the `service` field of `/api/health`. Renaming them would change stored data, sessions or published hashes, and nobody would see a difference.
- Names fixed on chain: the deployed programs' names and the metadata prefix `https://vicinity.city/coin-meta/` compiled into the deployed launchpad, which only a program upgrade changes. Also the on-chain Meteora partner entry: its name "Vicinity", which Meteora and Jupiter show, and its image address `/brand/vicinity-512.png`, which is still served for that reason (an S55 condition).
- `src/coinart-store.js` `artUrl`, which only works behind `COIN_V2_READY` (off).
- The root `/favicon.svg` and `/logo.svg`. Today's pages use them; no Citycoin.dev page links them.
- Provider settings: the Google OAuth consent-screen app name (E12), the Cloudflare resource names (Worker `vicinity-map`, D1 `vicinity-claims`) and the mail sender address (E10; the owner supplies it).
- `$VICINITY` itself. Its on-chain name cannot change (its metadata is not mutable), and holders keep their tokens. Under `BRAND` the site simply stops showing it.
- Git history and documents.

**What still names vicinity.city until citycoin.dev serves the site (CRAWL-5, F6).**
- The browser address bar.
- The first line and the URI of the wallet sign-in statement ("vicinity.city wants you to sign in…"). They are built from the serving host by design, so that a signature cannot be replayed on another host.
- The buttons in e-mails asked for on vicinity.city, the invite text and the in-app browser tip, which use the serving host. Since S06 an e-mail's button opens the host it was asked on, so an e-mail asked for on citycoin.dev opens citycoin.dev.

All of these follow `SITE_ORIGIN` and change by themselves at the cutover. To avoid them, turn the switch on together with `SITE_ORIGIN=https://citycoin.dev`, or after it.

**Terms of Use: the exact change the owner must approve before `BRAND=citycoin`.** The comparison is between the Terms page built with the freeze and `BRAND` unset, and the page built with `BRAND=citycoin` (the only way it ships). It was made block by block on the built pages, and again on the pages rendered in a browser (105 paragraphs on each side).

Name only: 19 paragraphs, plus the title, the subtitle, the closing line and the footer. The changes are "Vicinity" to "Citycoin.dev", "Vicinity Materials" to "Citycoin.dev Materials", "the Vicinity launchpad" to "the Citycoin.dev launchpad", and "vicinity.city" to "Citycoin.dev".

More than the name: 4 paragraphs. They named the old website address and the former X account. Amendment 01 makes that account a legacy link that is never shown as official, and the new X account is not verified yet (`amendment-01-new-main-token.md` section 4.4). So these paragraphs now point to the site's existing Feedback and support form. The exact text:

T1 (the opening paragraph). Before:

> These Website Terms of Use (these "Terms" or this "Agreement") are a contract between you and the operator of Vicinity (together with its affiliates, successors, and assigns, "Vicinity," "we," "our," or "us") (you and Vicinity each, a "Party," and together, the "Parties") and govern your access to and use of the website located at https://vicinity.city/ and all related pages and components (the "Website"), and such other content, features, and online services provided by Vicinity through the Website from time to time (collectively, the "Services"). Vicinity's legal identity and registered address are available on request by direct message to the Vicinity X account (@VicinityCitySOL) to any person with a legitimate need, including for the purpose of bringing or defending a legal proceeding.

After:

> These Website Terms of Use (these "Terms" or this "Agreement") are a contract between you and the operator of Citycoin.dev (together with its affiliates, successors, and assigns, "Citycoin.dev," "we," "our," or "us") (you and Citycoin.dev each, a "Party," and together, the "Parties") and govern your access to and use of the Citycoin.dev website and all related pages and components (the "Website"), and such other content, features, and online services provided by Citycoin.dev through the Website from time to time (collectively, the "Services"). Citycoin.dev's legal identity and registered address are available on request through the Feedback and support form on the Website to any person with a legitimate need, including for the purpose of bringing or defending a legal proceeding.

T2 (trademarks and infringement notices). Before:

> "Vicinity," the Vicinity name, and related logos, names, designs, and slogans are trademarks of Vicinity or its licensors; do not use them without our prior written permission. Other marks on the Website belong to their owners. If you believe Website content infringes your copyright or other rights, contact us via the Vicinity X account (@VicinityCitySOL) with a description of the material, its location, your contact information, and a good-faith statement that the use is unauthorized.

After:

> "Citycoin.dev," the Citycoin.dev name, and related logos, names, designs, and slogans are trademarks of Citycoin.dev or its licensors; do not use them without our prior written permission. Other marks on the Website belong to their owners. If you believe Website content infringes your copyright or other rights, contact us through the Feedback and support form on the Website with a description of the material, its location, your contact information, and a good-faith statement that the use is unauthorized.

T3 (informal resolution of disputes). Before:

> Informal resolution. Before filing a claim, try to resolve it by contacting us via the Vicinity X account (@VicinityCitySOL) with a description of your claim and the wallet address or transaction concerned. If we cannot resolve it within sixty days of receiving your first message, either Party may submit the Dispute to binding arbitration as below.

After:

> Informal resolution. Before filing a claim, try to resolve it by contacting us through the Feedback and support form on the Website with a description of your claim and the wallet address or transaction concerned. If we cannot resolve it within sixty days of receiving your first message, either Party may submit the Dispute to binding arbitration as below.

T4 (questions). Before:

> Questions about these Terms or the Services: contact us via the Vicinity X account (@VicinityCitySOL).

After:

> Questions about these Terms or the Services: contact us through the Feedback and support form on the Website.

Unchanged, for the owner to note:
- The Terms still list "city claiming, founder seats (including Seed Steward, Stake Ladder, and Squad Founding), founder elections and objections" among the Services (scripts/pages/src/terms.html:80), and the freeze closes those. The S16 review recorded this as S16-R3 (E12).
- The version line stays "Updated October 1, 2026 · Version 2026-10-01" (`TERMS_VERSION`, src/signup-core.js:19).

**Rules: the exact change.**

Against the Rules page built with the freeze and `BRAND` unset (S16's switch alone), the Citycoin.dev page changes only names:
- the page title ("Rules & fairness · Citycoin.dev");
- "It launches on the Citycoin.dev Launchpad";
- "Updates come from the city founder, the country manager or Citycoin.dev.";
- "your Citycoin.dev token and city-coin balances" (was "your $VICINITY and city-coin balances");
- "the ticker is fixed by the Citycoin.dev city list … shows the Citycoin.dev badge";
- the footer lines.

Against today's live Rules, it also carries S16's frozen copy, because `BRAND=citycoin` needs `LEGACY_SEATS=frozen`:
- The lead loses "No races, no single-person power, and no whale takeovers. Leadership is earned over time and chosen by locals."
- The City founders section becomes "Closed for changes": three lines (Seats, On record, Country managers) plus the coin line.
- The Stake Ladder, Seed Steward, Squad Founding and Country Managers sections are left out, and so is the "Who may vote or endorse" line.
- "Hide: a founder (their city), a manager…" becomes "Hide: a manager (their country) or an admin…".
- The one-account line loses ": that's why votes also need age and a check-in".
- "Sensitive actions (applying, endorsing, voting, moderating) need…" becomes "Sensitive actions, such as moderating, need…".

Two lines come from the scripts and the Worker at run time:
- The balance-check line says "The Citycoin.dev token is not live yet, so no balances are checked." (today: "Balance checks haven't recorded anything yet.").
- The never-list leaves out "Mint more $VICINITY: minting is switched off on the blockchain." (`/api/policy` lists 6 items).

The header stays "version 6 · in effect since 2026-10-16". The Founding Supporters rule (a 14-day average balance at a cutoff) stays word for word: it names no token, but it was written for `$VICINITY`.

**Owner decisions needed before `BRAND=citycoin`.** This pull request makes none of them.
1. Approve the Terms wording above (T1 to T4 and the name changes), or give other wording. For example, the contact could name the new X account once its verification record is complete.
2. Versions (F4). Either publish the Rules as version 7 and the Terms with a new version and date (members then accept the new Terms; `TERMS_VERSION`), or accept in writing that both keep their versions. The Rules page promises that "every rule change is a new version, announced first".

It is a neutral warning that the team named the account and its verification is not finished ("Final check follow-ups", finding 3). The alternatives are "not on the official list" (the earlier answer) or no special answer at all. Until the switch, the live checker (today's answers, unchanged by this pull request) calls that account "not us": "This X account is not us. Vicinity's official X: @VicinityCitySOL." (second check, RB-5).

Built: they are paused and say so (403 `founder_paused`, "Founder and manager actions are paused until the Citycoin.dev token is live. Nothing was changed."), and the Launchpad and the home page say new city coins launch once the token is live. So, from the switch until the token is live, no founder designs, chooses or launches a city coin, no country manager moderates (admins do), and no founder launches, even with an approval on record (the owner's approval round, which also read founders' balances, was deleted by S23); nothing is written and every seat and term stays as recorded. The alternative is S19's: no live-balance gate at all for a founder on record (the seat on record and the seat's own wallet), as master v5 acceptance A11 describes ("Recorded founder can launch later with zero main-token balance"); that changes who may act and needs its own review. Today the live site has 0 founder seats (section 2), so the choice affects nobody yet.

**Deploy checklist for this pull request (switched off).**
1. Confirm that `wrangler.jsonc` does not set `BRAND`. test/build-parity.test.js checks the committed build against it. Also confirm, by a read-only look, that the production Worker's dashboard variables have no `BRAND`. `keep_vars` keeps a dashboard value across deploys, but a dashboard-only `BRAND` changes nothing, because the pages were not built with it.
2. Bytes: the 16 built pages and `src/pages-built.js` equal `46c5825`'s. 23 scripts under `public/` change bytes (P1). `/brand/citycoin.css` and `/brand/citycoin.webmanifest` become reachable, but no page links them (P2).
3. Nothing changes in Cloudflare, DNS or D1: no route, setting or migration.
4. CI gains the `brand-switch-trial` job, which must pass alongside the build job.

**Switch-on order: when Citycoin.dev goes live on citycoin.dev.** No date can be given. Each step waits on the one before, and the owner holds several of them.
1. This pull request is reviewed and merged, with no visible change. It is built on `46c5825` (PR #102, PR #103 and PR #104, S06, included); if main moves again before the merge, it is rebased once more and the parity proof repeated against the new main. Until step 4 the live link checker keeps calling https://x.com/CityCoinDev "not us" (today's answer; owner decision 6).
2. The owner approves the Terms wording and decides the versions (decisions 1 and 2 above), and either supplies the sender (decision 3) or accepts e-mail off.
3. S06 is merged (PR #104, `46c5825`). Its deploy attaches citycoin.dev to the Worker while vicinity.city still serves, once the deploy token has the citycoin.dev zone (E1), and is then checked (S06 deploy checklist). This branch does not check the live state.
4. One configuration pull request sets `BRAND=citycoin`, `LEGACY_SEATS=frozen`, `DEPLOYMENT_ENV=production`, the new `EMAIL_FROM`, and `SITE_ORIGIN` and `SITE_ORIGINS` with `https://citycoin.dev`. It rebuilds the pages, passes CI (including `brand-switch-trial`, which writes nothing there: the committed `wrangler.jsonc` already carries the switch), and is merged and deployed. From that deploy, people on citycoin.dev see Citycoin.dev, and founder and manager actions are paused until the Citycoin.dev token is live (owner decision 7). For CI: the suite's stand-in takes back only the switch's own settings (`BRAND`, the freeze, `MAIN_TOKEN_V5`, the sender) and keeps `DEPLOYMENT_ENV` and `SITE_ORIGINS` as S06 committed them, so this pull request needs no test change for them; if it also sets `SITE_ORIGIN=https://citycoin.dev`, it updates S06's pin of that committed value in the same pull request (test/host-redirect.test.js, "vicinity.city stays canonical until S55"), because moving the canonical origin is a change the suite must see.
5. The full cutover (S55: the vicinity.city routes removed with no redirect, indexing on) waits on S58 (the legacy launch path closed) and the other S55 conditions.

**What this pull request changes.**
1. `wrangler.jsonc`: values only, with every line kept. Each new or changed line has a comment that names the owner's go and the value to put back.
   - `BRAND` = `citycoin` (was unset).
   - `LEGACY_SEATS` = `frozen` (was unset).
   - `LEGACY_LAUNCH` = `off` (was unset).
   - `WALLET_ONLY` = `on` (was unset). `LEGACY_LINK` stays unset on one condition: production had 0 members when the owner deleted all member data on 24 Oct 2026 (16:56 UTC), and vicinity.city has kept Google and e-mail sign-up open since then. So the pre-merge census (deploy checklist item 3) must find 0 Google- or e-mail-only accounts. If it finds any, this pull request sets `LEGACY_LINK=on` and a `LEGACY_LINK_UNTIL`, or the owner closes those accounts in writing (go-live review finding GL-SAFE-1).
   - `SITE_ORIGIN` = `https://citycoin.dev` (was unset).
   - `EMAIL_FROM` = `Citycoin.dev <noreply@citycoin.dev>` (was `Vicinity <noreply@vicinity.city>`).
   - Unchanged: `SITE_ORIGINS` (`https://citycoin.dev https://vicinity.city`; both hosts serve until S55), `DEPLOYMENT_ENV` (`production`), the routes (citycoin.dev, vicinity.city, www.vicinity.city), the one cron, and `FEED_V2` as the last setting. `HOST_REDIRECT` stays unset: no forwarding, by the owner's decision of 24 Oct 2026.
2. The pages, rebuilt from those settings. They carry the Citycoin.dev labels, S16's frozen copy and the new wallet-only copy. `src/pages-built.js` records `brand: "citycoin"`, `legacySeats: "frozen"` and `walletOnly: true`.
3. The wallet-only copy (new `scripts/pages/walletonly-copy.mjs`; S15 gate item 1). The page build applies it when `wrangler.jsonc` sets `WALLET_ONLY=on`, after the frozen copy and in the same pattern. Each passage is one exact whole line of the page source, and a passage that is not found exactly once stops the build. The copy is written for the Citycoin.dev pages only.
   - `/connect`: the description, the lead, the 1-2-3 steps and the account rule, as the page shows them before its script runs. `public/wallet-only.js` then writes the same words, as before.
   - The dashboard: the sign-up note under the example pass, and the pass's foot ("Google" becomes "Wallet").
   - The home FAQ: "How do I get my dashboard?" and "What do you store about me?". Since the review, also "I use FOMO (or another app wallet)": it now says to open the site in the wallet app's own browser, or to choose "Wallet on my phone" on a computer, instead of the tiny-transfer sign-in, which answers 404 (finding GL-2). Also "What if I don't live inside any city?" (an admin decides, finding GL-3) and "What is Citycoin.dev?" (no check-ins; new city coins launch once the token is live, findings GL-HON-6 and GL-6). The home page's and the dashboard's descriptions no longer name check-ins.
   - The dashboard's City tab: "Ask for a town near your city. An admin decides; approved towns join the map at the next update." and the button "Send request", in place of "Stand in the town", "Your Country Manager gives advice" and "Send (checks location)" (finding GL-3: under wallet-only sign-in no location is read and no manager holds a term). `/cities`: "Town missing? Ask for it from your dashboard". The dashboard's sign-up lead, its example pass and the member's role card say "memes and discussions" (finding GL-HON-6).
   - `/rules`, the city's coin: "new city-coin launches on this launchpad are closed until the Citycoin.dev token is live. Coins already launched keep trading …". `/app`: "Until then, city coins already launched trade right here in your browser. New city coins launch once the Citycoin.dev token is live." (finding GL-6).
   - `/rules`: the one-account line, "What we keep" and the member-profiles line.
   - `/terms`: section 5 (The Services), items (a) and (d), and section 6 (exact text below). The first version of this report said "section 2 (a)": section 2 of the published Terms is "Amendments to this Agreement" (finding GL-5).
   - The layout's account sheet: the Account entry reads "Wallet, sign-in and phone".
   - `/cities`: the founder requirement "Wallet + Google or e-mail" already went with S16's frozen copy, and nothing replaces it.
4. One rule ties the two switches together, so the Rules and Terms of edition 2026-10-25 are published only with the sign-in they describe. The page build refuses `WALLET_ONLY=on` without `BRAND=citycoin`, or with a configuration the Worker refuses (`pageWalletOnly`). It also refuses `BRAND=citycoin` without `WALLET_ONLY=on`, an accepted `DEPLOYMENT_ENV` and an accepted site configuration (`pageBrandPrerequisites`).
5. The editions (new `src/editions.js`). An edition belongs to the label set the deployed pages were built with (`src/brand.js` `brandOf`), never to a `BRAND` set only in the dashboard.
   - Today's pages: Rules version 6, in effect since 2026-10-16; Terms version 2026-10-01.
   - The Citycoin.dev pages: Rules version 7 and Terms version 2026-10-25, both in effect since 2026-10-25.
   - Every reader takes the version from there:
     - the Terms version a registration must accept and then records (`users.terms_version`; `src/register-core.js`, `src/auth.js`, `src/signup*.js`, `src/walletlink.js`);
     - `/api/me` (`termsVersion`, `policyVersion`);
     - `/api/policy` (`version` and `effectiveAt` under `BRAND`);
     - the policy number recorded with a seat, an election, a snapshot or an admin decision.
   - On the pages, the Rules header, the Terms version line and the dashboard's "rules version" carry brand blocks. Every built page carries `data-terms` on `<html>`. `public/site.js` reads it for the Terms bar and for the "I agree" on `/terms`, and the sign-up scripts fall back to it.
   - Old records stay as they were recorded, and stay readable. An account that accepted 2026-10-01 keeps that value, and `/api/me` reports it. The member confirms the current edition with the same call `/terms` uses (`POST /api/me/terms`). Nothing is rewritten.
6. One defect, found by the end-to-end run and fixed (`src/me.js` `badgesFor`). The dashboard's "Verified account" badge said "A verified Google login or e-mail." Under `WALLET_ONLY` it now says "An account opened with one free signature from its Solana wallet." The "On the streets" badge (check-ins on 3 days) is no longer offered to a member who has not earned it, because location once posts no new check-ins (S13). Since the review, the same goes for the badges nobody can earn (finding GL-4): "City Founder" and "Country Manager" while the legacy seats are frozen, and "Early member" under the Citycoin.dev labels. Its record (`users.early`) is set only before today's token launched, so no member of the Citycoin.dev site gets it. Earned badges stay, as records.
7. `public/_headers`. The `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` rule moves from `https://citycoin.dev/*` to `https://vicinity.city/*` and `https://www.vicinity.city/*`. citycoin.dev, the canonical site, is indexed: master section 06 says "Production is indexable as intended", and S53's `INDEXING` was never built as a switch. The copies on the old host stay out of search engines until S55 removes their routes.
8. CI and the suite.
   - `scripts/brand/switch-trial.mjs` (the `brand-switch-trial` job) now also expects `WALLET_ONLY=on` and `SITE_ORIGIN=https://citycoin.dev`. On the committed file it writes nothing and prints one line.
   - `test/helpers/today-build.mjs`, the suite's stand-in for today's site, also takes back `WALLET_ONLY`. It keeps `SITE_ORIGIN`, `SITE_ORIGINS`, `DEPLOYMENT_ENV` and `LEGACY_LAUNCH` as committed.
   - New `test/golive-config.test.js` (11 tests) checks the committed configuration as it is.
   - 11 test files had pins that this pull request moves on purpose: `SITE_ORIGIN` and `LEGACY_LAUNCH` committed, the editions, `data-terms`, the coupling of the two switches, the moved `_headers` rule, and one allowed import edge for `src/editions.js` in `test/persist-guard.test.js`. Each pin was updated.
9. `WALLET_ONLY` fails closed at run time too (go-live review finding GL-SAFE-2; `src/v5policy.js`). Over pages built wallet-only (`src/pages-built.js` `walletOnly: true`), a Worker whose `WALLET_ONLY` is missing, blank, `off` or a typo is held: `walletOnlyHeld` is true. Google, e-mail, password and tiny-transfer sign-in answer 404, sign-in, registration and pairing answer 503, a Google-only session reads as signed out, and the log says `wallet-only switch refused WALLET_ONLY_UNSET_PAGES_WALLET_ONLY`. A value outside `""`, `off` and `on` holds whatever the pages are (`WALLET_ONLY_VALUE_REFUSED`), the way `LEGACY_SEATS` does. Before this, any value other than exactly `on` reopened today's sign-in under the Citycoin.dev Terms. A rollback is not affected, because it rebuilds the pages without `walletOnly`.
10. The Terms acceptance records only the published edition (finding GL-HON-9; `src/me.js` `handleTermsAgree`). `POST /api/me/terms` takes `version` only when it equals the edition the deployed pages publish (`termsVersion`). Anything else, made up or older, answers 400 `bad_version` with `current`, and nothing is stored.
11. The balance history, at the deploy (finding GL-HON-7). Under `BRAND=citycoin` with no main token, the cron stops taking balance samples (its first run logs `"sample":{"sampled":false,"why":"not_launched"}`). With the rebrand branch's fix it also stops pruning: the `$VICINITY` days (`day:` blobs) and `balance_samples` rows taken since 24 Oct 2026 are kept whole, as history, until a main token counts (S17). The Founding Supporters text (decision 5) names a 14-day average at the snapshot, and that series is kept for it.

**Terms of Use: the exact wallet-only changes** (on top of T1 to T4). Section 5 (The Services), item (a). Before:

> (a) an informational and interactive website through which you may connect a third-party wallet and link a Google account or verified e-mail address;

After:

> (a) an informational and interactive website through which you may connect a third-party wallet and sign in with it;

Section 5 (The Services), item (d) (added by the go-live review, finding GL-HON-6; owner's go of 25 Oct 2026). Before:

> (d) community features such as memes, check-ins, discussions, badges, and holder verification;

After:

> (d) community features such as memes, discussions, badges, and holder verification;

Section 6 (accounts). Before:

> You may use the Services by proving control of a Solana wallet, and you may link a Google account or a verified e-mail address to that wallet. You are responsible for keeping your wallet, devices, and credentials secure; Citycoin.dev will never ask for your recovery phrase or private key, and anyone who does is not us. If someone else uses your linked accounts, you are responsible for what they do.

After:

> You may use the Services by proving control of a Solana wallet: you sign in by signing a free message with it, and your account is tied to that wallet. You are responsible for keeping your wallet, devices, and credentials secure; Citycoin.dev will never ask for your recovery phrase or private key, and anyone who does is not us. If someone else uses your wallet or your account, you are responsible for what they do.

The version line becomes "Updated October 25, 2026 · Version 2026-10-25". Still unchanged: section 5 (b) lists founder seats, Seed Steward, Stake Ladder and Squad Founding among the Services, although the freeze closes them (S16-R3; decision 5 keeps the passage).

**Rules: the exact wallet-only changes** (on top of the name changes and S16's frozen copy above).
- The one-account line. Before: "One account per wallet and per Google login or verified e-mail. It makes fake accounts harder, but no online check can prove someone is one unique person (one inbox can have several addresses)." After: "One account per wallet: you sign in with it. It makes fake accounts harder, but no online check can prove someone is one unique person (one person can have several wallets)."
- "What we keep". Before: "What we keep: your wallet address; with Google only the account id and first name; with e-mail the address itself (it is your account id) and a hash of the code (10-minute life); a made-up username; your home community; and, only if you add them, a contact e-mail and a phone number you can remove (the phone number is not verified and not used yet). We never keep a password anyone could read (an e-mail account that has one keeps only a salted hash of it), and check-in coordinates are never stored." After: "What we keep: your wallet address (it is how you sign in); a made-up username, or one you choose; your home community; and, only if you add it, a phone number you can remove (it is not verified and not used yet). There is no password to keep: you sign in by signing a free message with your wallet, never a transaction. Your coordinates are never stored."
- The member-profiles line, shown only while profiles are on. Before: "They never see your real name, how you signed in, your e-mail or phone, or your location." After: "They never see your phone number or your location."
- The city's coin (added by the go-live review, finding GL-6). Before: "The city's coin: the active founder designs it: its name, a short pitch, a colour and a logo. It launches on the Citycoin.dev Launchpad, on Meteora's bonding curve, paired with SOL. The ticker is the city's own, so nobody can copy it. Every design is public and every change is in the log. An admin approves it on chain before launch, and once it launches the design is locked for good." After: "The city's coin: new city-coin launches on this launchpad are closed until the Citycoin.dev token is live. Coins already launched keep trading, and a launched coin's design stays locked for good. The ticker is the city's own, so nobody can copy it, and every design and every change is public in the log."
- The header becomes "version 7 · in effect since 2026-10-25". The Rules promise that "every rule change is a new version, announced first"; this version is published together with the go-live, on the owner's go.

2. Log lines (PAR-02): with the committed settings the Worker counts both switches and logs no `wallet-only switch refused` line (end-to-end run). Since the review, the wallet-only line is also logged when the switch is held without being set on (GL-SAFE-2, item 9 above).
3. SEC-8: accepted (owner's go of 25 Oct 2026).
4. SEC-9: accepted (owner's go of 25 Oct 2026), with its condition.
5. PAR-03 (the optional moderator wording): not changed.
6. PAR-05 (`GET /api/me` answers 500 for a wallet member when every Solana RPC fails): not part of this pull request, and unchanged.
7. Contact e-mail (F1): under `WALLET_ONLY` members cannot add a contact e-mail (no contact-only sender is built). The new copy does not promise one: a phone number is the one optional contact detail.

**Mail under the committed settings.** Every route that sends a sign-in code, a password reset or the "Google sign-in was added" notice answers 404 under `WALLET_ONLY` with `LEGACY_LINK` unset. The wallet-linked notice goes only to an e-mail account's address or to a contact e-mail, and an account registered with its wallet has neither. So no mail goes to a member who registers after the go-live, and the unverified sender holds nothing up.

**What still says "vicinity" after the deploy, and why.**
- The vicinity.city host name, while that host serves: the address bar, and the first line and URI of the wallet statement there ("vicinity.city wants you to sign in…"). Both are built from the serving host by design. On citycoin.dev the statement names citycoin.dev.
- When asked on vicinity.city, the would-be e-mails' buttons and the wallet-app tip ("open its browser and type …") name vicinity.city, the host the request was served as. This is S06's design and is accepted (finding GL-7): sessions are bound to the host they were made on, so telling a member on vicinity.city to type citycoin.dev would not keep them signed in. Before any mail is switched on, the wallet-linked notice ("Remove it": unlink answers 404 under wallet-only sign-in) and the Google notice ("set a password") need wallet-only words. Under the committed settings neither is sent.
- Names nobody is shown (the rebrand's "Out of scope" list). The end-to-end run found two in the answers: the JSON key `"vicinity": null` in `/api/launchpad` and `/api/coins`, and the Worker's internal name `"service": "vicinity-map"` in `/api/health`.
- The hidden markup of today's sign-up states in `/connect` and on the dashboard, and today's words inside the shared scripts (`public/connect.js`, `public/signup.js`, `public/dashboard.js`). The scripts choose what to render from `/api/me` (`walletOnly: true`), and none of it is shown under `WALLET_ONLY`. The end-to-end run read every page signed out, signed in, and with JavaScript off. The markup stays because a rollback must serve today's site from the same scripts.

**Not closed by this pull request: owner actions.**
- `LEGACY_LAUNCH=off` closes the website's side only. A founder holding an unexpired Approval could still launch by calling vicinity_launchpad directly. Only the owner's `set_pause(launches = true)`, plus revoking every pending Approval, stops launches on chain (E3; `domain-cutover.md` section 4, "Before step 4"). The review made both blocking items before the merge, in S23's order (finding GL-ORD-10). Since 25 Oct 2026 the pause is a recorded deviation instead, taken under the owner's go of that day (deploy checklist item 6). No Approval exists to revoke, a launch needs one, and only the admin wallet can create one. The pause stays the owner's recommended step. What stays blocking is a fresh read-only re-check right before the merge, and any `Approval` found stops the merge.
- The census decision (deploy checklist item 3): if a Google- or e-mail-only account exists, the owner chooses the link window or a written closure.
- Resend: the owner verifies citycoin.dev as a sending domain before any feature sends mail from the new sender (E10).
- The new token, the official X and Telegram links and `MAIN_TOKEN_V5` are not changed here (E18).

**Tests and the end-to-end proof.** The rows are in section 11's tests table. In short:
- The full website command, with the review's follow-ups: exit 0; 2,722 tests, 2,721 pass, 0 fail, 1 skipped (the opt-in live Squads read). The first version gave 2,710 tests, 2,709 pass.
- `test/golive-review.test.js` (the follow-ups): 4 of 4 pass.
- `test/golive-config.test.js`: 11 of 11 pass.
- The CI trial on the committed file: one no-op line, exit 0.
- An end-to-end run of the first version's real Worker entry with its committed `wrangler.jsonc` and `public/`, in process, with every outside request refused: 348 of 348 checks pass, on citycoin.dev and on vicinity.city. That run was not repeated for the follow-ups. The behaviour they change is covered through the real routes by `test/golive-config.test.js` and `test/golive-review.test.js`, on both hosts where it applies. Every page (17 addresses) shows no "vicinity" and offers no Google, e-mail or password sign-in, whether read with JavaScript off, signed out or signed in. A new visitor registers with a fake wallet (the location once, Terms 2026-10-25, one signature) and sees the dashboard. The visitor then logs out and signs in again with the same wallet, with no second location check. The Google, e-mail, password and tiny-transfer routes answer 404, the legacy seat writers and the legacy launch writers answer 410, SOL and USDC quote from the fake Jupiter, and the job runs. Section 11 has the details.
