**Deploy checklist for this pull request.** Items 1 to 7 come before the merge, and each one is recorded in the pull request with its time. Items 2 to 6 are blocking. They were added by the go-live review (findings GL-SAFE-1, GL-SAFE-3, GL-SAFE-4, GL-ORD-10 and GL-DEP-11), because the first version of this checklist relied on facts recorded earlier that the live site can change. For the same reason the lead developer does items 2, 3, 4 and 6 again right before the merge, and the readings of 11:30 UTC (section 13) do not stand in for them. Since 25 Oct 2026 item 6 no longer waits for the owner's on-chain pause, which needs his signature. It is a fresh read-only re-check of the chain, and the pause is recorded there as a deviation taken under the owner's go of 25 Oct 2026, with its evidence.
1. Before the merge: main has not moved past `5ff441d` (the rebrand, PR #105, merged on 25 Oct 2026; this branch sits on it). If main has moved, rebase and run the full command again.
2. **Before the merge, blocking: citycoin.dev is attached and serving.** `curl -sI https://citycoin.dev/` answers 200 over a certificate issued for citycoin.dev, with S06's `x-robots-tag: noindex` (S06 is the version live then). The deploy token covers the citycoin.dev zone (E1). This session's own record says deploy run 97 failed at the citycoin.dev custom-domain step because the token lacks the zone. Until a re-run of S06's deploy attaches citycoin.dev, do not merge. If this pull request were deployed without the attach, `wrangler deploy` would upload the new version and then fail at the custom-domain step. The go-live version would then serve only on vicinity.city and www.vicinity.city, which it marks `noindex`, so the whole site would drop out of search. `SITE_ORIGIN`, the pages' `og:image` and the 421 `wrong_host` answer would point to https://citycoin.dev, which would not resolve.
3. **Before the merge, blocking, read only on the production D1: the census, re-done right before the merge.** The census of 11:30 UTC on 25 Oct 2026 (0 users; section 13) is not enough, because vicinity.city keeps Google and e-mail sign-up open until the deploy.
   - `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM users WHERE wallet IS NULL` gives the Google- or e-mail-only accounts made since the deletion of 24 Oct 2026.
   - The duplicate-wallet check of the S11 and S13 checklist (item 3): no two accounts share a wallet.
   - The usernames: every `handle` from `SELECT handle FROM users WHERE handle IS NOT NULL`, put through this branch's `src/me.js` `reservedUsername(handle, brandReserve({ BRAND: "citycoin", OFFICIAL_X_URL: "https://x.com/CityCoinDev" }))`, gives false. No existing name may pass for Citycoin.dev or @CityCoinDev (finding GL-1).
   - If the first count is above 0: set `LEGACY_LINK=on` and `LEGACY_LINK_UNTIL=<date>` in this pull request (the page build validates them), or record the owner's written decision to close those accounts.
   - If a name passes for the product: the owner decides before the merge whether to rename it in the admin console or keep it.
4. **Before the merge, blocking, right before the merge: a restore point, by the backup** (`schema-migrations.md` section 8.3, which names the switch-on of `cc5-identity` and S16's seat freeze). Run `node scripts/citycoin/backup.mjs --remote --execute --export <file outside the repository> --key-file <key> --yes` (bookmark, census, validated export), by the owner or the credential holder (E9). The script needs credentials that the release session does not hold. Its stand-in, as recorded for the census of 11:30 UTC (section 13), is a D1 Time Travel restore point. The UTC minute taken right before the merge is recorded in the pull request, and Time Travel can restore the production D1 to any minute of the last 30 days. After the deploy, WALLET_ONLY's first request creates its tables and columns in production D1 (`src/store.js`: `auth_challenges`, `sessions.auth`, `pairs.requester`, the four `users` columns, `signups.display_name`, `handoffs.bind`, `pairs.signup_id`, `pairs.bind` and the trigger `users_signup_completed_once`; the cron's cleanup can be that request). If the backup script ran, run the census again with the same key, and `--compare`. Every preserved category must be `same`, and the schema only `added`. With the Time Travel stand-in, run the read-only census of item 3 again after that first request, and record it next to the one taken before the merge. If neither the backup nor the restore point is recorded, do not merge.
5. **Before the merge, read only, in the Cloudflare dashboard: S06's full list of dashboard variables and secret names** (S06 deploy checklist item 3). None of these may be present, or each must be blank or `off`: `SITE_ORIGIN`, `BRAND`, `MAIN_TOKEN_MINT`, `OFFICIAL_X_URL`, `OFFICIAL_TELEGRAM_URL`, `LEGACY_LINK_UNTIL`, `SELECTION_ARCHIVE_URL_A`, `SELECTION_ARCHIVE_URL_B`, and every switch of `src/masterpolicy.js` `FLAG_VALUES`: `WALLET_ONLY`, `LEGACY_LINK`, `LEGACY_SEATS`, `SELECTION_V5`, `FOUNDER_RECORD_CHAIN`, `ACTIVITY_POINTS`, `MANAGER_APPS`, `METADATA_V5`, `LAUNCH_V5`, `LEGACY_LAUNCH`, `CC_TRADING`, `CC_HOURLY`, `HOST_REDIRECT`, `PUBLIC_V5`, `MAIN_TOKEN_V5`. Also check the secret names (`wrangler secret list`), including `GMAIL_USER`: a Gmail sender would send mail without Resend, which the page build's comment leaves to this checklist. `keep_vars` keeps a dashboard value across deploys. For the switches this pull request sets, the committed value replaces a dashboard value. With the committed configuration accepted, a dashboard `PUBLIC_V5=on` or `ACTIVITY_POINTS=shadow` would take effect.
6. **Before the merge, blocking: a fresh read-only re-check of the legacy launchpad, by the lead developer right before the merge.** No transaction, no key. On a public mainnet RPC, at `finalized`:
   - `getProgramAccounts` on the legacy launchpad `AkMP9qvAYmb8Vyu63Qhw8Z4ee1fgQbCRKkXWy5r5A1Ai`, once filtered by size 179 and once by the `Approval` discriminator `e90999310bde3b82`. Each must give 0 accounts.
   - The Launchpad PDA `4g9KRZ76Mpw6HCCu713yEGzm1txdCod3S1SugL7M9GwN` is read and its `launches_paused` value recorded, whatever it is.
   - `GET /official-coins.csv` answers 200 with an empty body, so no legacy city coin is launched or listed.

   The pull request records each value with its slot and UTC time. **If any `Approval` account exists, stop and do not merge.** With 0 there is nothing to revoke. `LEGACY_LAUNCH=off` closes only the website's side.

   **Recorded deviation (a): the owner's on-chain pause is not a pre-merge item.** S23's order, which the review put back here (finding GL-ORD-10), had the owner's wallet sign `set_pause(launches = true)` and revoke every pending Approval (E3) before the merge. The pause needs the owner's signature, and it has not been signed (`launches_paused = 0` at the read below). The lead developer goes ahead without it, under the owner's go of 25 Oct 2026, quoted as written: "I still see nothing on Citycoin.dev. Vicinity still active. What are you doing? Get them all sorted. Don't wait for my permission just go ahead and execute all the changes." Before either merge, the lead developer told the owner in writing, in the working conversation of 25 Oct 2026, quoted as written: "For step 3 I'm taking your message as written approval of two things. First, the old Vicinity logo address stored on-chain (the Meteora partner record) will stop loading. Second, I'm going ahead before the old launchpad is paused on-chain. That pause needs your signature. It's still worth doing, but nobody can launch through the old launchpad anyway: no launch approvals exist on-chain, and the website no longer builds launches." So this is a deviation taken under the owner's general go, and its consequence was stated to him in writing. The owner did not sign the pause, and his go does not name it. The statement's last clause, "the website no longer builds launches", holds from this pull request's deploy on: until then production has `LEGACY_LAUNCH` unset (the third reason below).

   Why the protection the pause was meant to give still holds:
   - **The program needs an approval for every launch.** `launch` takes an `Approval` account (seeds `["approval", city_id]`), closes it, and refuses while `launches_paused` is set (`solana/programs/vicinity-launchpad/src/instructions/launch.rs`). Only `approve_launch` creates an `Approval`, and it needs the Launchpad admin's signature (`approve_launch.rs`: `admin: Signer`, `has_one = admin`). The deployed program is the committed build: its bytes hash to SHA-256 `32906e19…c1621a`, the same as `solana/release/mainnet-2026-10-10/SHA256SUMS`, and it was last deployed at slot 455117076 (read below).
   - **The chain holds no approval and no coin:** 0 `Approval` accounts and 0 `Coin` accounts (read below).
   - **From this pull request's deploy on, the website builds no approval or launch:** with `LEGACY_LAUNCH=off` it builds and accepts no approval and no launch (410 `legacy_launch_closed`; S23). Production has `LEGACY_LAUNCH` unset until then: main `5ff441d` does not set it, and the live `/api/me` carried no `legacyLaunch` key at 15:20 UTC on 25 Oct 2026. Until the deploy has finished, the live admin City coins tab can still build an `approve_launch`, which only the admin can sign (residual risk below).
   - **No coin is launched:** `/official-coins.csv` is empty.

   **Residual risk, stated plainly.** The admin wallet `13qRam63xqqd8KNUoAmHWQu7ro71oHqEsYHYaG5MsRiN` (the owner's) could still create an approval by signing `approve_launch` outside the website. The program's upgrade authority, the Squads vault `AB5qB1iAukPB7p1dkukQ1qSke3yQBDeSd5o69dLFGs7X` (2 of 3), could change the code. The pause would not stop either of them: the admin can unpause, and an upgrade can change anything. A legacy coin launched that way would carry `https://vicinity.city/coin-meta/` for good (rebrand-inventory.md P02). Until this pull request's deploy has finished, including the minutes between the fresh re-check and the end of the deploy, the live site's admin City coins tab can still build an `approve_launch`; only the admin can sign it, so this adds nothing beyond the admin's own ability above. The fresh re-check right before the merge is what catches an approval. **The pause stays a recommended owner step** (E3, S58). Admin, City coins still builds it while `LEGACY_LAUNCH=off`, because only resuming is closed.

   **Values read for this record.** Read on 25 Oct 2026 between 14:08 and 14:10 UTC, read-only, on `https://api.mainnet-beta.solana.com` at `finalized`. These readings are evidence for the deviation. They are not the pre-merge re-check, which is done again right before the merge.
   - The Launchpad PDA (slot 461285542; block time 14:08:17 UTC): admin `13qRam…sRiN`, no pending admin, `launches_paused = 0`, `payouts_paused = 0`.

   - The ProgramData account `A6xsFatq4XE5GDXvQws7u2A1fx2WwBKVL8cutYVo3N1a` (read from the program account; the same address `src/sol/pda.js` derives): upgrade authority `AB5qB1…Gs7X` (slot 461285544). The program bytes hash to SHA-256 `32906e19…c1621a`, last deployed at slot 455117076 (read at slot 461285677).
   - The Squads multisig `6v8hwKYSAWXXx6ZGGmvbg87n8eVy9MAgNruFgVqRFjYV` (slot 461285759): threshold 2 of 3 members, time lock 0, no config authority. Its vault 0 is `AB5qB1…Gs7X`.
   - `GET https://vicinity.city/official-coins.csv` (14:08:28 UTC): 200, `text/csv; charset=utf-8`, 0 bytes. `GET https://vicinity.city/api/official-coins` (14:08:29 UTC) answered `coins: []`.
7. Before the merge: the go-live review's findings are closed or accepted as recorded below ("Go-live review follow-ups").
8. Right after the deploy, within minutes, on both hosts:

**Rollback.** One pull request that puts the previous values back. Change the values and never delete a line: `keep_vars` would keep a deleted line's value in Cloudflare.
- `BRAND` = `""`
- `LEGACY_SEATS` = `"on"`
- `LEGACY_LAUNCH` = `"on"`
- `WALLET_ONLY` = `"off"`
- `SITE_ORIGIN` = `""`

Then rebuild the pages (`node scripts/pages/build.mjs`) and commit them together with `src/pages-built.js`, in the same pull request. Pages still built wallet-only would keep the Worker held (item 9 of "What this pull request changes"), with every sign-in answering 503. `test/golive-config.test.js` checks that these values build today's pages and record again. Nothing is deleted: accounts registered in the meantime keep their rows. Sessions and accounts from before the go-live also come back (finding GL-HON-5). Unexpired sessions not made by a wallet proof count again, and Google- and e-mail-only accounts can sign in again, because the switch-on read them as signed out and never revoked them. Their recorded Terms version (2026-10-25) then differs from today's pages (2026-10-01), so the Terms bar asks them to confirm today's text. It blocks nothing.


### Stage S55: citycoin.dev only, the vicinity.city routes removed (branch `cc/s55` on `cc/golive` `31213b8`; this pull request, built, not merged)

**The owner's words.** The decision of 24 October 2026 (`domain-cutover.md` section 0, quoted as written): "i dont want any domain forward from vicinty. leve that blank both. vicinity.city and vicinitycity.com i wil need them for other purpose." The go of 25 October 2026 (quoted as written): "I still see nothing on Citycoin.dev. Vicinity still active. What are you doing? Get them all sorted. Don't wait for my permission just go ahead and execute all the changes." S55 is the last step of the domain cutover (`domain-cutover.md` section 3, step 4), built on that go. In S55 text, "this pull request" means the S55 one. **The lead developer's decision (25 October 2026), recorded as made.** Under the owner's delegation, after the two messages above, the lead developer decided that the S55 gates which need the owner's signature or his specific words no longer block S55: each becomes a recorded deviation taken under the owner's go of 25 October 2026, with its evidence and the reason the protection it was meant to give still holds (below, "Recorded deviations (a), (b) and (c)"). Before either merge he told the owner in writing, in the working conversation on 25 October 2026 (quoted as written): "For step 3 I'm taking your message as written approval of two things. First, the old Vicinity logo address stored on-chain (the Meteora partner record) will stop loading. Second, I'm going ahead before the old launchpad is paused on-chain. That pause needs your signature. It's still worth doing, but nobody can launch through the old launchpad anyway: no launch approvals exist on-chain, and the website no longer builds launches." Its last clause holds from the go-live's deploy on, which comes before the S55 merge (deploy checklist item 1). He will also tell the owner, in the same conversation before the S55 merge, that every old vicinity.city link, bookmark and the Google sign-in callback on vicinity.city stop answering and that members sign in again on citycoin.dev with the same wallet; that message is not sent yet, and sending it is a blocking item of the deploy checklist below. Before this, `acceptedHost` accepted the legacy hosts whatever `SITE_ORIGINS` listed (master section 04's 12-month rule), so with S55's settings a vicinity.city request that still reached the Worker would have been served as the site, bound to vicinity.city, and www.vicinity.city would have answered 301 to vicinity.city. With `SITE_ORIGINS` listing vicinity.city (S06, the go-live, S55's rollback) every answer is as before. The dropped `HOST_REDIRECT` code (`src/hostroute.js`, never to be set) uses the same `acceptedHost`, so it too would refuse an old name once vicinity.city is not served, never 308 it to citycoin.dev. **Deploy checklist for S55.** Items 1 to 4 come before the merge, each done by the lead developer right before the merge and recorded in the pull request with its time, and all four are blocking. (History: the first version made only two items blocking; after the S55 review, findings H1 to H3, five items were blocking, among them the on-chain pause, the owner's specific acceptance of the partner logo going dark, and his written acknowledgement; the lead developer's decision moved those three to recorded deviations and added the fresh re-check and his own message.) None of this delays S55 today: it cannot merge before PR #106, which waits on the owner's token change.

Not part of this item: the code e-mail, "Continue with Google" and "vicinity.city answers exactly as before", which the go-live replaced (that note), and the S54-probe line, which is not met because S54 is not built (`domain-cutover.md`, the status at the top, item 7) and blocks nothing. One pull request that reverts the S55 code: `git revert --no-commit <the S55 merge commit on main>` (on this branch before the merge, the S55 code commit `62984da`, built as `6e7e611`, then `e37da6e`; the review follow-up commit `4601d0b`, was `0647e01`; and the commit that put the amendment in force, `ba54d77`, was `ffefa61`), then `git checkout HEAD -- '*.md'` so the documents keep the S55 record, with a dated line on why. It restores together `SITE_ORIGINS` (`https://citycoin.dev https://vicinity.city`), both custom domains after the citycoin.dev route, the two `noindex` host rules, `src/site-config.js`, `src/official.js` (the old names official again), the post-deploy check, the amendment in both files and the tests that pin each; it changes values and deletes no line, so `keep_vars` is no obstacle. Not by changing `SITE_ORIGINS`, the routes and `public/_headers` alone (S55 review finding S55-DEPLOY-1): the build that runs before every deploy runs every test, and such a pull request fails them (rehearsed below), so its deploy uploads nothing. Before it, the deploy token gets the `vicinity.city` zone back (`docs/DEPLOY.md` step 1): without it wrangler uploads the new version and then fails to attach vicinity.city, and the run goes red. Rolling the go-live back needs this first (the page build refuses `SITE_ORIGIN` "" with S55's `SITE_ORIGINS`).
